I just know I end up missing things either way, so I couldn't really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Does this problem with not catching what people are saying happen in all situations, or is it worse in certain places, like at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it seems like it happens pretty much everywhere. I haven't really noticed it being any worse at the plant compared to being at home with my wife. It's just... no matter where I am or who's talking to me, I always seem to end up missing pieces of what folks are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting distracted easily when others are talking to you?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure if I'd call it getting distracted. I don't really notice anything else pulling my attention away, like a noise or something happening in the room. It's more like... I'll be looking right at the person, and my brain just sort of stops registering the words. So I couldn't really tell you if I'm actually getting distracted by something or what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to tasks or activities that you need to complete?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. I mean... if I have a job to do, like my work on the line at the plant or fixing something around the house, I just do it. I haven't really noticed having any trouble paying attention to the actual work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse or do things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I can't really think of a time where I just did something without thinking about it first. I usually just go about my day, do my work, come home... I don't really do things on impulse, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. If I’m sitting in a safety huddle at work, or even just at the dinner table, my leg is always bouncing. My hands are usually messing with a pen or jingling my keys, too. Folks actually tell me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I just have a hard time sitting completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could focus and understand what people are saying if you really tried to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I do try. But... honestly, that's actually what brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. My supervisor at the plant or my wife will be talking straight to me, and a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So even when I am trying, I just seem to miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders off because you're not interested in what the other person is saying?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F88, R41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Well, what brought me in is that people keep telling me I'm not listening to them. It's getting to be a real problem. My supervisor at the plant, or my wife at home, they'll be talking straight to me, and... I don't know, a minute later I realize I only caught about half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. It's causing a lot of friction, so I figured I needed to come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to focus on and understand what's being said when it's something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I... I haven't really noticed if it makes a difference whether I'm interested or not. I just know I end up missing things either way, so I couldn't really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Does this problem with not catching what people are saying happen in all situations, or is it worse in certain places, like at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it seems like it happens pretty much everywhere. I haven't really noticed it being any worse at the plant compared to being at home with my wife. It's just... no matter where I am or who's talking to me, I always seem to end up missing pieces of what folks are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting distracted easily when others are talking to you?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure if I'd call it getting distracted. I don't really notice anything else pulling my attention away, like a noise or something happening in the room. It's more like... I'll be looking right at the person, and my brain just sort of stops registering the words. So I couldn't really tell you if I'm actually getting distracted by something or what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to tasks or activities that you need to complete?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. I mean... if I have a job to do, like my work on the line at the plant or fixing something around the house, I just do it. I haven't really noticed having any trouble paying attention to the actual work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse or do things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I can't really think of a time where I just did something without thinking about it first. I usually just go about my day, do my work, come home... I don't really do things on impulse, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. If I’m sitting in a safety huddle at work, or even just at the dinner table, my leg is always bouncing. My hands are usually messing with a pen or jingling my keys, too. Folks actually tell me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I just have a hard time sitting completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could focus and understand what people are saying if you really tried to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I do try. But... honestly, that's actually what brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. My supervisor at the plant or my wife will be talking straight to me, and a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So even when I am trying, I just seem to miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders off because you're not interested in what the other person is saying?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's causing a lot of friction, so I figured I needed to come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to focus on and understand what's being said when it's something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I... I haven't really noticed if it makes a difference whether I'm interested or not. I just know I end up missing things either way, so I couldn't really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Does this problem with not catching what people are saying happen in all situations, or is it worse in certain places, like at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it seems like it happens pretty much everywhere. I haven't really noticed it being any worse at the plant compared to being at home with my wife. It's just... no matter where I am or who's talking to me, I always seem to end up missing pieces of what folks are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting distracted easily when others are talking to you?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure if I'd call it getting distracted. I don't really notice anything else pulling my attention away, like a noise or something happening in the room. It's more like... I'll be looking right at the person, and my brain just sort of stops registering the words. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Well, what brought me in is that people keep telling me I'm not listening to them. It's getting to be a real problem. My supervisor at the plant, or my wife at home, they'll be talking straight to me, and... I don't know, a minute later I realize I only caught about half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. It's causing a lot of friction, so I figured I needed to come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to focus on and understand what's being said when it's something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I... I haven't really noticed if it makes a difference whether I'm interested or not. I just know I end up missing things either way, so I couldn't really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Does this problem with not catching what people are saying happen in all situations, or is it worse in certain places, like at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it seems like it happens pretty much everywhere. I haven't really noticed it being any worse at the plant compared to being at home with my wife. It's just... no matter where I am or who's talking to me, I always seem to end up missing pieces of what folks are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting distracted easily when others are talking to you?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure if I'd call it getting distracted. I don't really notice anything else pulling my attention away, like a noise or something happening in the room. It's more like... I'll be looking right at the person, and my brain just sort of stops registering the words. So I couldn't really tell you if I'm actually getting distracted by something or what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to tasks or activities that you need to complete?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. I mean... if I have a job to do, like my work on the line at the plant or fixing something around the house, I just do it. I haven't really noticed having any trouble paying attention to the actual work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse or do things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I can't really think of a time where I just did something without thinking about it first. I usually just go about my day, do my work, come home... I don't really do things on impulse, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. If I’m sitting in a safety huddle at work, or even just at the dinner table, my leg is always bouncing. My hands are usually messing with a pen or jingling my keys, too. Folks actually tell me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I just have a hard time sitting completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could focus and understand what people are saying if you really tried to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I do try. But... honestly, that's actually what brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. My supervisor at the plant or my wife will be talking straight to me, and a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So even when I am trying, I just seem to miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders off because you're not interested in what the other person is saying?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an easier time listening and understanding if the topics of conversation were more interesting to you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it matters how interesting the topic is. Even if it's something I really care about, like... I don't know, talking about my favorite sports team or something I like doing on the weekends, I still have the same issue. My mind just wants to go somewhere else, or I feel like I need to be moving or doing something. So, yeah, the topic doesn't really change how hard it is to just stand there and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus better if someone was speaking directly to you in a quiet room with no distractions?\n\nPatient: I don't think a quiet room would fix it, honestly. Even if it's just me and one other person and there's no noise, I'm still... I still just feel that urge to get up, you know? Like my motor is running. I might not have a TV or other people distracting me, but my own body just wants to be moving. So no, I don't think the quiet room really makes a difference for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble listening because you're just not interested in what the other person is talking about?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's about being interested. Actually, that’s kind of the main thing that brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. \n\nMy supervisor on the floor, or my wife... they’ll be talking straight to me, looking right at me, and... a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. I haven't really noticed having any trouble paying attention to the actual work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse or do things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I can't really think of a time where I just did something without thinking about it first. I usually just go about my day, do my work, come home... I don't really do things on impulse, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. If I’m sitting in a safety huddle at work, or even just at the dinner table, my leg is always bouncing. My hands are usually messing with a pen or jingling my keys, too. Folks actually tell me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I just have a hard time sitting completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could focus and understand what people are saying if you really tried to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I do try. But... honestly, that's actually what brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. My supervisor at the plant or my wife will be talking straight to me, and a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So even when I am trying, I just seem to miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders off because you're not interested in what the other person is saying?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an easier time listening and understanding if the topics of conversation were more interesting to you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it matters how interesting the topic is. Even if it's something I really care about, like... I don't know, talking about my favorite sports team or something I like doing on the weekends, I still have the same issue. My mind just wants to go somewhere else, or I feel like I need to be moving or doing something. So, yeah, the topic doesn't really change how hard it is to just stand there and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus better if someone was speaking directly to you in a quiet room with no distractions?\n\nPatient: I don't think a quiet room would fix it, honestly. Even if it's just me and one other person and there's no noise, I'm still... I still just feel that urge to get up, you know? Like my motor is running. I might not have a TV or other people distracting me, but my own body just wants to be moving. So no, I don't think the quiet room really makes a difference for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble listening because you're just not interested in what the other person is talking about?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's about being interested. Actually, that’s kind of the main thing that brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. \n\nMy supervisor on the floor, or my wife... they’ll be talking straight to me, looking right at me, and... a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So it's not that I don't care about what they're saying, I just somehow miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering instructions or tasks that you're supposed to do later?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My memory is actually pretty fine. \n\nIf I hear what I'm supposed to do, I'll remember to do it later. Like I was saying, the main issue is just catching the whole instruction in the first place because I miss parts of the conversation. But once I actually know what the task is, I don't forget it. So no, remembering things later isn't really a problem for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My supervisor at the plant or my wife will be talking straight to me, and a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So even when I am trying, I just seem to miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders off because you're not interested in what the other person is saying?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an easier time listening and understanding if the topics of conversation were more interesting to you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it matters how interesting the topic is. Even if it's something I really care about, like... I don't know, talking about my favorite sports team or something I like doing on the weekends, I still have the same issue. My mind just wants to go somewhere else, or I feel like I need to be moving or doing something. So, yeah, the topic doesn't really change how hard it is to just stand there and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus better if someone was speaking directly to you in a quiet room with no distractions?\n\nPatient: I don't think a quiet room would fix it, honestly. Even if it's just me and one other person and there's no noise, I'm still... I still just feel that urge to get up, you know? Like my motor is running. I might not have a TV or other people distracting me, but my own body just wants to be moving. So no, I don't think the quiet room really makes a difference for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble listening because you're just not interested in what the other person is talking about?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's about being interested. Actually, that’s kind of the main thing that brought me in here. Like I was saying, the main issue is just catching the whole instruction in the first place because I miss parts of the conversation. But once I actually know what the task is, I don't forget it. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an easier time listening and understanding if the topics of conversation were more interesting to you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it matters how interesting the topic is. Even if it's something I really care about, like... I don't know, talking about my favorite sports team or something I like doing on the weekends, I still have the same issue. My mind just wants to go somewhere else, or I feel like I need to be moving or doing something. So, yeah, the topic doesn't really change how hard it is to just stand there and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus better if someone was speaking directly to you in a quiet room with no distractions?\n\nPatient: I don't think a quiet room would fix it, honestly. Even if it's just me and one other person and there's no noise, I'm still... I still just feel that urge to get up, you know? Like my motor is running. I might not have a TV or other people distracting me, but my own body just wants to be moving. So no, I don't think the quiet room really makes a difference for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble listening because you're just not interested in what the other person is talking about?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's about being interested. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So it's not that I don't care about what they're saying, I just somehow miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering instructions or tasks that you're supposed to do later?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an easier time listening and understanding if the topics of conversation were more interesting to you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it matters how interesting the topic is. Even if it's something I really care about, like... I don't know, talking about my favorite sports team or something I like doing on the weekends, I still have the same issue. My mind just wants to go somewhere else, or I feel like I need to be moving or doing something. So, yeah, the topic doesn't really change how hard it is to just stand there and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus better if someone was speaking directly to you in a quiet room with no distractions?\n\nPatient: I don't think a quiet room would fix it, honestly. Even if it's just me and one other person and there's no noise, I'm still... I still just feel that urge to get up, you know? Like my motor is running. I might not have a TV or other people distracting me, but my own body just wants to be moving. So no, I don't think the quiet room really makes a difference for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble listening because you're just not interested in what the other person is talking about?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's about being interested. Actually, that’s kind of the main thing that brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. \n\nMy supervisor on the floor, or my wife... they’ll be talking straight to me, looking right at me, and... a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So it's not that I don't care about what they're saying, I just somehow miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering instructions or tasks that you're supposed to do later?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My memory is actually pretty fine. \n\nIf I hear what I'm supposed to do, I'll remember to do it later. Like I was saying, the main issue is just catching the whole instruction in the first place because I miss parts of the conversation. But once I actually know what the task is, I don't forget it. So no, remembering things later isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could repeat back what someone just told you, word for word, if you really focused on it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed having any trouble paying attention to the actual work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse or do things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I can't really think of a time where I just did something without thinking about it first. I usually just go about my day, do my work, come home... I don't really do things on impulse, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. If I’m sitting in a safety huddle at work, or even just at the dinner table, my leg is always bouncing. My hands are usually messing with a pen or jingling my keys, too. Folks actually tell me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I just have a hard time sitting completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could focus and understand what people are saying if you really tried to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I do try. But... honestly, that's actually what brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. My supervisor at the plant or my wife will be talking straight to me, and a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So even when I am trying, I just seem to miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders off because you're not interested in what the other person is saying?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I want to hear what my wife is saying, and I definitely need to know what my supervisor wants me to do on the line. It's not like I don't care or I'm just ignoring them. My mind just kind of... drifts off anyway, even if it's something I really need to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're putting in enough effort to listen when others are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean... I'm looking right at them, trying to lock in on the words. I don't know what else I'm supposed to do to try harder, honestly. It takes a lot of energy just to stand there and try to block everything else out. So, yeah, the effort is definitely there on my end. It just... doesn't seem to make a difference in what actually sticks in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to put your thoughts into words when trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might talk a little fast sometimes, or maybe jump around a bit when I'm telling a story, but I don't really struggle to find the words. If I need to explain something, I just say it. I don't feel like I get stuck trying to figure out how to put it into words.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your brain is working harder than normal when you're trying to listen to someone and understand what they're saying?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say my brain is working harder than normal... I mean, I don't really know what normal is for anyone else. It's just... it's just how it is for me. I try to listen, but I don't feel like I'm straining my brain or anything like that. It's more about trying to stay still and keep my attention on them, rather than my brain physically working harder to understand the words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an easier time listening and understanding if the topics of conversation were more interesting to you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it matters how interesting the topic is. Even if it's something I really care about, like... I don't know, talking about my favorite sports team or something I like doing on the weekends, I still have the same issue. My mind just wants to go somewhere else, or I feel like I need to be moving or doing something. So, yeah, the topic doesn't really change how hard it is to just stand there and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus better if someone was speaking directly to you in a quiet room with no distractions?\n\nPatient: I don't think a quiet room would fix it, honestly. Even if it's just me and one other person and there's no noise, I'm still... I still just feel that urge to get up, you know? Like my motor is running. I might not have a TV or other people distracting me, but my own body just wants to be moving. So no, I don't think the quiet room really makes a difference for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble listening because you're just not interested in what the other person is talking about?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's about being interested. Actually, that’s kind of the main thing that brought me in here. People just keep saying I’m not listening to them. \n\nMy supervisor on the floor, or my wife... they’ll be talking straight to me, looking right at me, and... a minute later I realize I only caught half of what they said. Then I end up having to ask them to say it all over again. So it's not that I don't care about what they're saying, I just somehow miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering instructions or tasks that you're supposed to do later?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My memory is actually pretty fine. \n\nIf I hear what I'm supposed to do, I'll remember to do it later. Like I was saying, the main issue is just catching the whole instruction in the first place because I miss parts of the conversation. But once I actually know what the task is, I don't forget it. So no, remembering things later isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could repeat back what someone just told you, word for word, if you really focused on it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't think I've ever tried to do it word for word. I mean... if I could actually force myself to just lock in and focus that hard, maybe I could? But like I was saying, keeping that focus in the first place is the whole problem. So, yeah, I don't really know if I'd be able to do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to pay attention the longer someone is talking to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed if it's about how long they talk, to be honest. It feels like I can miss what they're saying right at the beginning just as easily as I do a few minutes in. So... no, I wouldn't really say the length of the conversation makes a difference. It just kind of happens whenever.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to understand and recall what someone said if you read it in writing instead of hearing it?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I don't get a lot of written instructions at work—mostly it's just guys talking to me on the floor. So I haven't really had a chance to compare if reading it is better than hearing it. \n\nI mean, maybe it would be, since it's right there in front of me and I could look at it again, but... yeah, I can't say for sure if it actually makes a difference for me. I just haven't really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to understand and recall what someone said if you had a chance to hear it again, or if it was explained in a different way?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I guess if they repeated it a bunch of times or broke it down differently, maybe it would stick better? But it's hard to say. Usually, if I miss it the first time, getting them to say it again just feels like... I don't know, it's just more talking. I haven't really noticed if hearing it a second time or in a different way actually fixes the problem.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
